### 52. ★★★☆☆ S&P 500 Futures Gain After Warsh’s Remarks; Chip Stocks Extend Slide - Yahoo Finance

- **分类**: 🤖 AI/科技
- **来源**: GN Markets
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-16T10:39:35+00:00
- **发生了什么**: S&amp;P 500 Futures Gain After Warsh’s Remarks; Chip Stocks Extend Slide&nbsp;&nbsp;Yahoo Finance

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

S&P 500 Futures Gain After Warsh’s Remarks; Chip Stocks Extend Slide
Wall Street sign in lower Manhattan New York by Stuart Monk via Shutterstock December S&P 500 E-Mini futures (ESZ26) are up +0.14% this morning as sentiment improved after Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh avoided signaling his near-term policy intentions at an IMF meeting.
Mr. Warsh participated in a roughly half-hour fireside chat with International Monetary Fund Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva at the IMF's meeting in Bangkok on Friday morning. Still, he offered no guidance on near-term monetary policy. "I don't pre-judge meetings, I don't believe in saying now what I might do at the meetings that we have in the balance of this year… It's a new chapter at the Fed, and we're not in that business anymore," Warsh said.
Bill Gates Says Despite Elon Musk Being Mean to Him, He's Still 'In Awe' of His Work — 'He's Brilliant, He's Rich, He's Influential'
'I Can Support My Family By Working Three Days A Week': Jeff Bezos Says Al Gains Will Be So Big Families Won't Need 2 Full-Time Earners
At 97, Charlie Munger Borrowed $37 Million to Go All-In on Alibaba — 2 Years Later He Called It One of the 'Worst Mistakes' of His Life
Tired of missing midday reversals? The FREE Barchart Brief newsletter keeps you in the know. Sign up now!
Traders took Warsh's remarks as another sign the Fed is unlikely to rush into raising rates at its meeting later this month. U.S. rate futures were pricing in a 14.9% chance of an October hike, down from 28% a day earlier.
"The most important thing was what [Warsh] didn't say. Markets took the lack of hawkishness as good news," according to Deutsche Bank's Jim Reid.
On the negative side, chip and AI infrastructure stocks remained under pressure in pre-market trading, failing to join the rebound among their Asian and European peers. Micron Technology (MU), Sandisk (SNDK), and Intel (INTC) each fell more than -1%.
Meanwhile, the price of WTI crude was little changed on Friday as traders weighed plans by the International Energy Agency to speed up the release of emergency oil reserves against lingering Middle East supply risks.
Investors are now awaiting the Federal Reserve's industrial production report.
In yesterday's trading session, Wall Street's major indices ended mostly higher as investors rotated out of chipmakers and into a wider range of stocks as well as bonds. Software stocks climbed, with Intuit (INTU) and ServiceNow (NOW) rising over +6%. Also, defensive healthcare stocks gained, with Alnylam Pharmaceuticals (ALNY) climbing over +4% and Eli Lilly (LLY) rising more than +3%. In addition, International Paper (IP) surged over +6% and was among the top percentage gainers in the S&P 500 after the company said it was in discussions with potential buyers for its Europe, Middle East, and Africa packaging business following unsolicited approaches. On the bearish side, chip and AI infrastructure stocks slid, with Applied Materials (AMAT) and Lam Research (LRCX) dropping more than -3%.
Market participants also parsed a flurry of U.S. economic data on Thursday. The U.S. producer price index for final demand rose +1.3% m/m and +6.3% y/y in September, stronger than expectations of +0.6% m/m and +5.3% y/y. Also, the core PPI, which excludes volatile food and energy costs, rose +0.7% m/m and +5.0% y/y in September, above expectations of +0.3% m/m and +4.4% y/y. In addition, U.S. retail sales rose +0.8% m/m in September, stronger than expectations of +0.3% m/m, and core retail sales, which exclude motor vehicles and parts, rose +0.9% m/m, stronger than expectations of +0.6% m/m. Finally, the number of Americans filing initial jobless claims in the past week rose by 1K to 199K, in line with expectations.
"Producer-price inflation firmed in September as higher energy prices translated to higher transportation costs and wholesalers recovered margins," said Troy Durie of Bloomberg Economics. "More important for the Fed, PPI components that feed into PCE—namely airfares—came in above expectations, putting more pressure on PCE inflation."
Today, investors will focus on U.S. industrial production and manufacturing production data, set to be released in a couple of hours. Economists project industrial production to rise +0.4% m/m and manufacturing production to rise +0.3% m/m in September, compared with August's figures of no change m/m and -0.3% m/m, respectively.
The U.S. import and export price indexes will also be released today. Economists expect the import price index to climb +0.9% m/m and the export price index to jump +1.0% m/m in September, compared with the previous month's figures of +0.7% m/m and +0.6% m/m, respectively.
On the earnings front, property and casualty insurer Travelers (TRV), along with regional banks Truist Financial (TFC), M&T Bank (MTB), Citizens Financial (CFG), and Regions Financial (RF), are scheduled to report quarterly results today. According to Bloomberg Intelligence, S&P 500 companies are expected to post an average +25% jump in third-quarter earnings from a year earlier.
In the bond market, the yield on the benchmark 10-year U.S. Treasury note is at 5.10%, up +0.49%.
The Euro Stoxx 50 Index is up +0.69% this morning as investors regained their appetite for risk at the end of a turbulent week. Technology stocks were among the biggest gainers on Friday, tracking a modest rebound in their Asian peers. The sector also drew support from chip-equipment maker Aixtron (AIXA.D.DX), which climbed more than +5% after reporting stronger-than-expected Q3 order intake and reaffirming its full-year guidance. In addition, defense, industrial, and automobile stocks climbed. The benchmark index was on track for a weekly gain. Final data from Eurostat released on Friday showed that the Eurozone's annual inflation rate picked up to +3.8% in September, confirming preliminary estimates. Separate data showed that the Eurozone's trade balance swung into a deficit in August as higher energy imports and weaker trade in machinery and vehicles weighed on the balance. Meanwhile, Eurozone government bond yields rose on Friday, moving away from multi-week lows hit in the previous session as the rebound in equities dampened demand for safe-haven assets. In other corporate news, Nordex (NDX1.D.DX) jumped over +13% after the German wind-turbine maker posted upbeat preliminary Q3 EBITDA and raised its full-year EBITDA margin guidance.
Eurozone's CPI and Core CPI data were released today.
Eurozone's September CPI rose +0.6% m/m and +3.8% y/y, in line with expectations.
Eurozone's September Core CPI rose +0.2% m/m and +2.5% y/y, in line with expectations.
Asian stock markets today closed in the green. China's Shanghai Composite Index ($CHSC) closed up +0.24%, and Japan's Nikkei 225 Stock Index ($NKY) closed up +1.39%.
China's Shanghai Composite Index closed slightly higher today after authorities took steps to shore up the stock market by urging companies and state funds to buy shares while guiding some investors to limit selling. Real estate, gold-related, and defense stocks led the gains on Friday. Still, the benchmark index posted losses for the week. Bloomberg News reported that financial regulators convened a meeting on Thursday to discuss measures aimed at stabilizing the market. Authorities also provided informal guidance to several major insurers, directing them to disclose their equity holdings, avoid realizing losses on underwater positions, and align investment decisions with broader economic objectives. In addition, regulators urged the so-called national team to step up stock purchases. Meanwhile, at least 10 AI supply-chain companies, including optical transceiver maker Eoptolink and chip-equipment maker Piotech, announced share buyback plans. A series of technology companies, including chipmakers MetaX Integrated Circuits and Hygon Information Technology, also issued Q3 profit forecasts pointing to rapid growth. In addition, turnover surged in several exchange-traded funds favored by the national team during late-session trading on Friday. Investor attention now turns to China's third-quarter gross domestic product data, due Monday.
Japan's Nikkei 225 Stock Index closed higher today as investors snapped up shares after a recent selloff. Overnight gains on Wall Street supported sentiment, which was further buoyed after Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh stopped short of signaling greater urgency to tighten policy despite this week's hotter-than-expected inflation data. Nearly all sectors ended higher on Friday, with technology and financials among the biggest gainers. Still, the benchmark posted a steep weekly loss. Meanwhile, Japanese government bonds were little changed on Friday, steadying after the previous session's rally as risk-off sentiment that had driven investors into safe-haven assets eased. Elsewhere, foreign investors bought a net 3.52 trillion yen worth of Japanese stocks in the week ended Oct. 10, marking the largest weekly inflow since Apr. 11, according to Ministry of Finance data. In corporate news, BayCurrent jumped over +17% after the consulting firm announced plans to use excess cash for investments and share buybacks. The Nikkei Volatility Index, which takes into account the implied volatility of Nikkei 225 options, closed down -10.96% at 32.17.
Chip and AI infrastructure stocks slipped in pre-market trading. Micron Technology (MU), Sandisk (SNDK), and Intel (INTC) each fell more than -1%.
Tesla (TSLA) gained over +3% in pre-market trading after the EV maker unveiled its new Roadster sports car.
Fox Corporation (FOXA) rose more than +3% in pre-market trading after BofA double-upgraded the stock to Buy from Underperform with a price target of $85.
Netflix (NFLX) slid more than -2% in pre-market trading after naming Peter Rice to succeed Bela Bajaria as chief content officer, effective Oct. 26.
Equifax (EFX) fell over -1% in pre-market trading after Oppenheimer downgraded the stock to Perform from Outperform.

### 53. ★★★☆☆ The Stock Market Is Doing Something for Only the 3rd Time in 156 Years, and History Is Clear a Crash May be Hiding in Plain Sight - The Motley Fool

- **分类**: 📈 市场
- **来源**: GN Markets
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-16T13:30:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: The Stock Market Is Doing Something for Only the 3rd Time in 156 Years, and History Is Clear a Crash May be Hiding in Plain Sight&nbsp;&nbsp;The Motley Fool

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

The S&P 500 ( ^GSPC -1.09% ) has been on an impressive run since the bear market of 2022. Since hitting a bottom in October 2022, the index has more than doubled, gaining approximately 115%. This translates into an annualized return of roughly 21% over four years, more than twice its historical average of approximately 10% annually, including dividends.
Here&#x27;s the catch: Stock prices do not outperform their long-term averages indefinitely. Eventually, markets experience mean reversion, a process in which unusually strong returns are followed by periods of weaker performance. While this doesn&#x27;t guarantee a crash is imminent, one particular valuation indicator is flashing a warning investors should not ignore.
The S&P 500 CAPE ratio is entering dangerous territory
The cyclically adjusted price-to-earnings ratio (CAPE) measures the S&P 500&#x27;s price relative to its average inflation-adjusted earnings over the last 10 years. The point is to smooth out temporary earnings fluctuations, providing a more balanced perspective on whether stocks are expensive or fairly valued.
Historically, the CAPE ratio has averaged around 17.8. But as of October 2026, readings are hovering around 41. To appreciate how unusual this is, consider some history.
Before the 1929 stock market crash, the CAPE ratio approached 33. The Dow Jones Industrial Average ( ^DJI -0.34% ) subsequently lost 89% of its value between 1929 and 1932.
In more recent history, the CAPE ratio exceeded 40 in 1999 and peaked near 44 before the dot-com bubble collapse. From March 2000 through October 2002, the S&P 500 declined 49%, while the Nasdaq Composite ( ^IXIC -2.15% ) plunged 77%.
Today&#x27;s CAPE reading above 40 puts stock valuations in rare territory, suggesting the current market is historically expensive. Much like the internet revolution, generative AI promises to transform entire industries, leading investors to pay premiums for companies expected to dominate the future. But I see one important difference between today&#x27;s AI boom and the dot-com bubble.
During the late 1990s, investors poured unprecedented sums into speculative technology companies with little revenue, no profits, and untested business models. Today&#x27;s AI leaders are considerably different.
Nvidia ( NVDA -1.43% ) sells the graphics processing units (GPUs) that power much of the AI revolution. Despite its stock price appreciation over the last few years, Nvidia&#x27;s earnings have also expanded as demand for its chips continues to accelerate.
Meanwhile, Micron Technology ( MU -5.63% ) is benefiting from soaring demand for high bandwidth memory (HBM) and advanced DRAM solutions. Alphabet ( GOOGL -2.47% ) ( GOOG -2.32% ) has combined an established, highly profitable advertising business with an expanding cloud infrastructure and AI services ecosystem.
These businesses aren&#x27;t selling investors a vision of the future. They&#x27;re actually generating consistent AI-driven revenue and profits today. This is why their forward price-to-earnings (P/E) ratios look reasonable relative to what the broader market&#x27;s CAPE ratio might suggest.
The crack I see is that future growth in the AI landscape hinges on the overarching assumption that big tech will continue spending at historical levels on AI infrastructure. According to Morgan Stanley , capital expenditures (capex) among the five major hyperscalers -- Microsoft , Amazon , Alphabet, Meta Platforms , and Oracle -- are projected to approach $800 billion in 2026, climb to $1.2 trillion in 2027, and reach $1.4 trillion in 2028.
Those investments are an enormous opportunity for Nvidia, Micron, and other semiconductor suppliers. But they also create a financial opportunity cost. Every dollar AI hyperscalers spend building data centers is one that cannot also be used to fund share repurchases or dividend increases. If AI infrastructure investments fail to deliver attractive returns on aggressive timelines, Wall Street could eventually question whether these budgets were justified.
The combination of outsize gains over the last four years fueling historically elevated valuations suggests the market has less room for disappointment. This is why taking some profits right now could make sense. In particular, for investors whose portfolios have become heavily concentrated in AI-related technology stocks, trimming some of your biggest winners could prove wise.
Of course, selling everything would be a questionable decision. Instead, I&#x27;d consider maintaining exposure to AI through a more diversified cohort of businesses. Industrial companies stand to benefit from data center construction, electrical equipment, and automation, while energy companies can profit from rising demand for electricity and natural gas. Meanwhile, utilities should benefit from investments in power generation and transmission infrastructure.
The biggest mistake investors can make right now is assuming that historically expensive valuations mean a crash is right around the corner. The CAPE ratio is a useful indicator of long-term expected returns, but it does not have a track record of predicting precisely when corrections will occur.

### 54. ★★★☆☆ State regulators approve nearly $140M rate hike for Consumers Energy natural gas customers - ClickOnDetroit | WDIV Local 4

- **分类**: 🛢️ 能源
- **来源**: GN Energy
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-16T14:21:58+00:00
- **发生了什么**: State regulators approve nearly $140M rate hike for Consumers Energy natural gas customers&nbsp;&nbsp;<font color="#6

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

2 warnings and 9 advisories in effect for 11 regions in the area
State regulators approve nearly $140M rate hike for Consumers Energy natural gas customers
The Michigan Public Service Commission has approved nearly $800 million in rate hikes for Consumers Energy since 2020
State regulators approved a $139.2 million annual natural gas rate increase for Consumers Energy — down from the utility’s original request for $240 million
Starting Nov. 1, Consumers Energy customers will see a 4.3% rate increase on their natural gas bills, amounting to approximately $4 per month
Michigan Attorney General Dana Nessel intervened in the case, urging the Commission charged with approving the rate hike to “slash the request by more than half”
Consumers Energy customers in Michigan will be paying more on their utility bills starting next month after state regulators approved a 4.3% rate increase on Thursday.
Under the new rate — which begins on Nov. 1 — households will pay an average of $4 more for natural gas each month, according to the Michigan Public Service Commission (MPSC).
The latest rate hike will allow the utility to collect an additional $139.2 million per year from its 1.8 million natural gas customers across the state — a 42% reduction from the $240 million Consumers Energy initially requested. The MPSC also approved a 9.8% return on equity, the utility’s profit margin on capital investments.
The utility says the increase is necessary to support investments in the safety and reliability of its natural gas system, and to support projects to replace aging gas mains and service lines.
However, the Michigan Attorney General’s office says it found “several instances” within Consumers Energy’s rate hike application where the company “inflated capital spending projections and included for themselves unreasonable profit margins.”
Related->> Michigan lawmakers push to rein in energy rates, hold utility companies accountable
Michigan Attorney General Dana Nessel said in a news release on Thursday that she urged state regulators to slash Consumers’ original rate hike request by more than half, arguing that the company shouldn’t be permitted to request a rate hike above 3.5% due to the burden it places on families already facing rising energy costs.
“Utility customers across the State, whether they pay their bill to Consumers Energy or DTE, ask all the time when their energy bills will stop climbing, and it’s clear that the answer is never,” Nessel said. “For nearly eight years my office has worked doggedly to curb these utility rate hikes, and in turn the cases have only grown. Change must come to our current system of annual on-demand rate hikes and our regulatory structure, but the only changes we’ve seen are fatter rates and heavier bills.”
Nessel says recent rate hike requests from DTE and Consumers Energy have included “inappropriate” expenses such as “private jet travel for staff, millions in executive incentive compensation tied to maximizing shareholder returns, and other unsupported expenditures that could not be demonstrated to be reasonable or prudent.”
The MPSC says it did not approve costs that couldn’t be “sufficiently justified,” noting that it did not approve Consumers’ $7.9 million request for transmission pipeline integrity work, citing “a pattern of over-projecting of costs and under-spending on this program.”
State regulators have approved nearly $800 million in rate hikes for Consumers Energy since 2020, according to Nessel’s office. Other rate hike cases currently open before the MPSC include DTE’s electric rate hike request and Upper Peninsula Power Company’s electric rate hike request .

### 56. ★★★☆☆ Intel and AMD Drop 3% as Rotation Out of AI Chip Stocks Continues; NVIDIA Holds Steady - 24/7 Wall St.

- **分类**: 🤖 AI/科技
- **来源**: GN AI
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-16T14:54:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Intel and AMD Drop 3% as Rotation Out of AI Chip Stocks Continues; NVIDIA Holds Steady&nbsp;&nbsp;24/7 Wall St.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Intel and AMD Drop 3% as Rotation Out of AI Chip Stocks Continues; NVIDIA Holds Steady
Investors are bailing on AI chip stocks, but not all of them equally, and the gap between winners and losers reveals something important about how Wall Street is rethinking the cost of building out artificial intelligence infrastructure.
Published October 16, 2026, 10:54am ET · 3 min read
A rotation out of AI-linked chip stocks isn't letting up, and two of the biggest names in processors are falling again. Intel ( NASDAQ:INTC | INTC Price Prediction ) stock is down 3% to $92.60 in morning trading. Meanwhile, Advanced Micro Devices ( NASDAQ:AMD ) stock is down 3% to $544.74, matching the decline in Intel shares.
Chip stocks are falling harder than the rest of large-cap tech. The iShares Semiconductor ETF ( NASDAQ:SOXX ) is down 2%, a smaller drop than Intel and AMD shares. By comparison, the Invesco QQQ Trust ( NASDAQ:QQQ ) is down 0.9%, a far softer slide than the chip fund.
NVIDIA ( NASDAQ:NVDA ) is another AI chip designer and the leader for the group. Its Blackwell Ultra systems and the newly ramping Vera Rubin platform power a Data Center business that sells to many of the same cloud providers Intel and AMD serve. NVIDIA shares are up 0.4% to $218.10, so not every chip stock is down today.
The decline extends a rotation out of AI-linked chip stocks as investors question how AI infrastructure spending gets financed . Server processors from Intel and AMD feed that same expansion, which puts both stocks near the center of the trade.
Heading into the decline, Intel has improving results. Intel's latest quarterly report posted what CEO Lip-Bu Tan called "our strongest revenue growth in more than fifteen years." That same report showed its foundry business posting a large operating loss, a reminder of how costly its manufacturing push remains.
Data center chips anchor Intel's AI business, with Xeon server processors handling cloud, enterprise and AI infrastructure workloads. Beyond servers, Intel has also demonstrated rack-scale AI systems with SambaNova and Foxconn for inference and agentic AI tasks. Its Client Computing and Physical AI Group adds processors for PCs, edge AI and robotics.
Contract manufacturing is what separates Intel from its biggest rivals. Intel designs chips and is building a contract manufacturing business that requires heavy spending, grounded by process technologies such as Intel 3 and Intel 18A. Rivals AMD and NVIDIA design chips and outsource production, which leaves the cost of building factories to their manufacturing partners.
Spending plans at Intel reflect that commitment. The company is increasing investment in equipment, clean room space and substrates, and it's expanding capacity for Xeon processors built on Intel 3. Washington backs that effort too, with Intel receiving U.S. CHIPS Act funding that supports its domestic production.
AMD built its AI franchise on an outsourced production model. Its Data Center lineup includes EPYC server processors, Instinct accelerators and Helios rack-scale systems, and that division now generates the majority of AMD revenue. Anthropic plans to deploy MI450 Series GPUs in Helios racks, while other large AI customers are scaling Instinct deployments.
Beyond the data center, Ryzen processors lifted AMD client sales, and embedded demand strengthened across several end markets. On the gaming side, AMD's business shrank over the same stretch, leaving the company more dependent on AI and server demand.
Intel's revenue recovery drives the optimistic view, with the company reporting this result: Data center and foundry sales both grew in the latest quarter, while client computing also expanded. Intel's gross margin improved sharply from a year earlier as well, giving the turnaround a stronger financial base.
Foundry spending is the main worry for Intel. Its foundry unit is still running a large operating loss.
Strong AI growth stories at Intel and AMD sit alongside real near-term pressure from the rotation. On balance, Intel brings a recovering business with heavy capital needs, while AMD offers a lighter production model tied closely to AI accelerator demand. Shareholders of these chip stocks, especially INTC, should consider keeping their position sizes modest until the foundry question at Intel settles.

The Nasdaq $NDAQ Composite slid roughly 2% during Friday afternoon trading, with semiconductor and artificial intelligence names facing fresh selling. The

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Nasdaq drops about 2% as chip and AI stocks slide, while the Dow falls 0.5%
Oil prices rise above $100 a barrel as investors trim bets on an October Fed rate hike and weigh AI debt concerns
By Cris Tolomia · 2 min read · Updated October 16, 2026
The Nasdaq $NDAQ Composite slid roughly 2% during Friday afternoon trading, with semiconductor and artificial intelligence names facing fresh selling. The Dow Jones Industrial Average fell 0.5%, and the S&P 500 lost about 1.2%.
The Nasdaq is on pace for a weekly loss of more than 4%, which would be its largest since June, according to The Wall Street Journal . The Dow is up less than 1% for the week.
Semiconductor stocks extended their decline. The VanEck Semiconductor ETF has dropped 8% this week, putting it on track for its worst week since July, according to CNBC . Micron $MU Technology stock has fallen about 10% this week after CXMT, a Chinese company, announced progress toward next-generation memory technology, according to The Wall Street Journal.
Concern over tech borrowing for AI buildout is weighing on neocloud stocks. CoreWeave stock has lost more than 23% this week, and its total debt has risen above $51 billion, according to CNBC.
Michael Rosen, chief investment officer at Angeles, said increased debt issuance deserves monitoring if it reaches excessive levels, but that "we&#x27;re not remotely close to that right now," according to CNBC.
Energy stocks gained, with Chevron $CVX among them, which limited the Dow&#x27;s losses. Oil prices climbed on heightened friction pitting the U.S. against Iran and Russia against Ukraine. West Texas Intermediate futures climbed 2%, topping $100 a barrel, while Brent futures added 3% and moved beyond $109.
Wholesale inflation climbed this week by the most since 2022, according to CNBC, and consumer price growth continues to run above the central bank&#x27;s 2% goal.
Fed Chair Kevin Warsh addressed an IMF gathering in Bangkok during U.S. overnight hours but gave no indication of whether the central bank planned a rate increase ahead of the year&#x27;s close. Investors scaled back wagers on a rate increase in October after his remarks, according to The Wall Street Journal.
The CME $CME Group&#x27;s FedWatch tool puts the odds at 85% that the Fed holds its overnight rate steady when policymakers meet Oct. 27-28, according to CNBC.

<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Nvidia stock trims losses as chip sell-off eases amid AI financing concerns
Chip stocks pared losses Thursday after concerns over debt-heavy balance sheets triggered a broader AI-driven sell-off on Wall Street.
The overall Tech ( XLK ) sector trimmed declines as investors rotated into defensive sectors and bought up Treasurys, pushing long-dated yields substantially lower.
Shares of Nvidia ( NVDA ) and peer Advanced Micro Devices ( AMD ) fell roughly 1%, paring earlier losses. , after a global chip stock sell-off, as seen on AlphaSpace's heatmap below.
Chip flash memory maker Sandisk ( SNDK ), which has been the S&P 500's ( GSPC ) top performer in 2026, pared losses to close just below the flatline.
Nvidia stock sinks as chip sell-off deepens on hot inflation data
AI trade rout sparks flight to Treasurys, bond yields sink: Chart of the Day
Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang addresses CoreWeave stock plunge: 'I'm optimistic'
Memory highflier Micron ( MU ) turned positive throughout the session, while SK Hynix ( SKHY ) fell 1%. Both stocks were hit earlier this week over worries about Chinese competition.
Positive earnings and record profit from chip contractor TSMC ( TSM ) weren't enough to lift the stock. Shares of the largest contract chip manufacturer pared losses to drop less than 1%.
Worries over the cost of capital for companies with heavy debt on their balance sheets have collided with hotter-than-expected inflation data, sparking fresh concerns over how rising prices may affect the Federal Reserve's next move.
Meanwhile, a spike in bond yields for Nvidia-backed CoreWeave ( CRWV ) has raised concerns about the neocloud's debt-heavy balance sheets and rising interest rates amid sticky inflation.
CoreWeave shares erased a 7% drop on Thursday, closing up more than 5% after Nvidia ( NVDA ) CEO Jensen Huang expressed optimism about the data center operator following a 15% rout on Wednesday.
Ines Ferre is a senior business reporter for Yahoo Finance.

- **分类**: 🤖 AI/科技
- **来源**: GN AI
- **多源确认**: 1

- **发生了什么**: Stocktwits AI Roundup: AI Chip Selloff Deepens, CoreWeave Debt Concerns And Apple’s China AI Launch
Home News Markets Equity Stocktwits AI Roundup: AI Chip Selloff Deepens, CoreWeave Debt Concerns And Apple’s China AI Launch
Stocktwits AI Roundup: AI Chip Selloff Deepens, CoreWeave Debt Concerns And Apple’s China AI Launch
Mounting concerns about AI investment rattled chipmakers and infrastructure

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Stocktwits AI Roundup: AI Chip Selloff Deepens, CoreWeave Debt Concerns And Apple’s China AI Launch
Home News Markets Equity Stocktwits AI Roundup: AI Chip Selloff Deepens, CoreWeave Debt Concerns And Apple’s China AI Launch
Stocktwits AI Roundup: AI Chip Selloff Deepens, CoreWeave Debt Concerns And Apple’s China AI Launch
Mounting concerns about AI investment rattled chipmakers and infrastructure providers this week.
Representative image of an automated robotic arm gripping a CPU inside of a circuit board factory. (Photo by luza studios/Getty Images)
CoreWeave plunged 15% on Wednesday, triggered by a drop in its bond prices.
Apple reportedly plans to launch Apple Intelligence in China in December.
A sharp selloff in chip and AI-linked stocks dominated the conversation this week, spilling over into markets across Asia despite strong quarterly results from TSMC and ASML. Investors also grew wary of the financial health of neocloud operators, while reports suggested Apple was finally gearing up to launch its AI features in China.
U.S. semiconductor stocks fell sharply in the week amid fresh fears over the sustainability of AI spending and high valuations of major players in the sector. Concerns over CoreWeave’s plummeting bond prices, a Gartner report warning about the future of neocloud businesses, and hotter-than-expected inflation fueled the selloff.
The iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) shed 10.2% in the week through Thursday, with Intel (INTC) and Micron (MU) falling over 9%.
CoreWeave shares slumped as investors scrutinized the debt financing behind its AI infrastructure expansion. CRWV  plunged 15.5% on Wednesday after Bloomberg reported that the company’s unsecured debt due in 2032 fell below 80 cents on the dollar, a level widely seen as the threshold for financial strain. Shares recovered on Thursday, although the weekly drop stood at 15%.
Anthropic IPO Valuation Expectations Ease To $1.5 Trillion
Some Anthropic investors are  considering a valuation of around $1.5 trillion for the AI startup’s anticipated IPO, below the earlier expectation of $2 trillion. Slowing sequential revenue growth and rising AI financing costs have prompted investors to reassess the company’s valuation.
Strong results and upbeat outlooks from Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMC) and ASML Holding (ASML) eased concerns about a potential slowdown in AI chip demand, setting a positive tone ahead of hyperscalers’ earnings reports in the coming weeks.
ASML  raised its 2026 sales outlook for a third time, while TSMC  projected a 51% revenue increase in the ongoing quarter. Their shares still fell amid the broader chip selloff.
China Approves Alibaba AI Models For Apple’s iPhones
Apple Inc. is gearing up to release its artificial intelligence platform in mainland China, after it received approval from Beijing after a lengthy process, Bloomberg reported.
The iPhone maker is planning to launch Apple Intelligence in the region in December as part of an upcoming iOS 27.3 update, according to Bloomberg’s sources, although the launch will exclude the new Siri AI assistant.
Shares of AI infrastructure firms Intuit, Inc. (INTU) and Shopify, Inc. (SHOP) were among the top gainers in the Nasdaq-100, rising 9.6% and 8.3%, respectively.
The Invesco QQQ Trust Series 1 (QQQ) fell 3.1%, while Rocket Lab Corp. (RKLB) plunged 19.2%, emerging as the biggest loser in the week.

### 60. ★★★☆☆ Future of western security is at stake in Yemen civil war, says country’s former PM

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: Guardian World
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-16T04:00:50+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Maeen Abdulmalik Saeed says Iran-backed militia’s use of drones represents ‘new era of radical terrorism’Food and fuel run low as besieged Yemeni city braces for Houthi breakthroughThe future of western security is being fought out in the mountains and hills surrounding the south-western Yemeni city of Taiz, the country’s former prime minster has said.<

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Houthi supporters gather in the militia’s stronghold city of Sana’a, Yemen, earlier this month. Photograph: Mohammed al-Mahdi/Reuters
Houthi supporters gather in the militia’s stronghold city of Sana’a, Yemen, earlier this month. Photograph: Mohammed al-Mahdi/Reuters
Future of western security is at stake in Yemen civil war, says country’s former PM
Maeen Abdulmalik Saeed says Iran-backed militia’s use of drones represents ‘new era of radical terrorism’
Food and fuel run low as besieged Yemeni city braces for Houthi breakthrough
The future of western security is being fought out in the mountains and hills surrounding the south-western Yemeni city of Taiz, the country’s former prime minster has said.
Maeen Abdulmalik Saeed, now an adviser to Yemen’s president, said it would be catastrophic if the city fell to the Iran-backed Houthi rebels, since control of the strategic Bab al-Mandab strait in the Red Sea and the road to Aden – the site of the UN-recognised government – would be lost.
Appealing for more western help, Saeed said he had no doubt that the Houthis intended to seize control of all of Yemen, and claimed their goal was “to help Iran to expand the chaos”.
Houthis ride in a funeral procession for their fellow fighters in Sana’a, Yemen, in September. Photograph: Yahya Arhab/EPA Saeed admitted the lack of coordination among government-backed troops, competition between Gulf states and a slowness to appreciate the implications of Yemen acquiring long-range drone technology had contributed to the pressure facing his government, and its chief backer Saudi Arabia .
“We only have light weapons and Saudi Arabia did not plan at an early stage for this escalation, but it is never too late to regain our position,” he said.
Saeed called on the west to think through the implications of allowing the Houthis to take control of Yemen, arguing the country would never be secure with Houthis on the land.
‘We expect war to reach the city soon’: Yemenis stockpile as Houthis advance on Taiz
Saeed is not only an adviser to the Yemeni president, Rashad al-Alimi, he was prime minister between 2018 and 2024 and a former negotiator with the Houthis.
He said: “The battle around Taiz is not just a battle for Taiz, but a broader battle that involves the whole of the western world, and one of the most important maritime straits. The Houthis know controlling Taiz is controlling the straits and it’s the highlands before the straits and the coastal city of Mocha and the Gulf of Aden. If Taiz is lost, access to the strait will be lost. The rest of the country will come later. The Houthi plan is to control the whole of Yemen.”
Taiz has become the new frontline in the war between the Houthis and Yemeni government forces after the Houthis launched a military campaign last month to take Yemen’s Red Sea coast, ending four years of relative calm in Yemen after a UN-brokered ceasefire froze the frontlines in 2022 .
He said existing intelligence showed the Houthis were already seeking to control parts of the south, including the former Soviet camp at al-Sabiha, in the Lahij governorate, and eventually Aden. “The Houthis bleed a lot,” he said, “they suffer a lot, but they always want quickly to use their gains or any collapse to gain more land.”
With some Houthi leaders offering safe passage to armed groups to leave the city, he said for now the Houthis were allowing some fuel and commodities into the urban area of Taiz which houses more than 500,000 people, but in the meantime they were expanding their control of rural areas and smaller major cities.
Saeed said: “In a matter of days there will be a full siege and they will try to enter the city. I know the Houthis. They will show some flexibility until they are ready to control. It will be much worse than the siege of 2015. It will be catastrophic.”
He described Taiz as a modern democratic city untied to tribal culture, and predicted the Houthis would try to impose their Zaydi education, financial and tax systems very quickly, but the citizens would continue to resist. “The loss of Taiz would have a very significant symbolic impact on the idea of the modern state in Yemen and of resisting the Houthi project which relies on tribal networks and extremist religious education.”
Displaced Yemeni families from al-Kadha, west of Taiz, at al-Akdaf refugee camp toward the end of last month. Photograph: Abdulnasser Alseddik/AP Saeed admitted Taiz only had light weapons and the government had not been prepared for the loss of the west coast, allowing the Houthis to attack Taiz from four different axes.
“We have good troops,” he said, “but the problem is the coordination. We needed more communication between the National Shield Forces, National Resistance Forces and the Giants Brigades. It’s been a wake-up call inside the government and the army because not enough happened between the truce of 2022 and the restart of the fighting this year to reorganise the troops. The Houthi ideology solely is to fight.”
He added: “I hope now that the knife has come to our neck, and the Houthis represent a critical threat, the priority would be to come together to defeat the Houthis, and then to discuss other demands and guarantees.”
The exiled leader of the southern secessionist force, Aidarous al-Zubaidi, on Tuesday called for a mobilisation of southern troops against the Houthis, but coupled this with a demand for a referendum on secession within two years. He brought tens of thousands on to the streets in support on Wednesday, but his return would be a bitter pill for Riyadh to swallow.
Saeed said it was not a coincidence that the Houthi attack along the Red Sea coast in September had come when Iran was under heavy pressure.
“Its airplanes and airports cannot operate, 90% of the oil was coming out of Hormuz. That is why they attacked the Saudi east-to-west pipelines. The Houthis, by talking about a Saudi blockade, have found a reason to hit the Saudi oil installations, airports and they want to do it now because the only thing Iran can do is to keep expanding the consequences of the war for America.”
He said Houthi military capability and negotiating power had been transformed over the last decade leading to its ability to target key sites in Saudi Arabia now. “In 2017, the Houthis had nothing but blind missiles to fire at us that missed their targets.” By 2020, he said intelligence existed that Iranian experts operating from the Houthi capital Sana’a were training Houthis and in December of that year, in a devastatingly effective attack, they fired ballistic missiles at Aden airport. The attack left 28 dead and 107 injured, including Saeed himself, newly installed as prime minister. The missile travelled 186 miles (299km).
He explained: “We thought even then they did not have that many missiles, but since then the Iranians have expanded their manufacturing lines.” In January 2022, using missiles, the Houthis attacked Abu Dhabi oil tanker trucks more than 900 miles away.
In October 2022, inside Yemen, they struck the Nushayma oil terminal and al-Dhabba port, severely damaging government finances. By the time the US attacked Yemen in March 2025, he said “it was clear the Houthis had dozens of targeted missiles, land sea missiles and drones. They had become a unified front with Iran, and developed their missile base.”
He warned: “This is a new era of radical terrorism. It is not the old chaotic one of Isis and al-Qaida. It is semi-state actors and militias threatening powerful states with cheap technologies, and smuggled third-party components. The way Iran has invested in the Houthis could become a model for threatening navigation elsewhere. It is a new model of war, without traditional armies, needing different air defences. Gulf countries have to reshape and rethink their defences”.

</details>
